353	        "notes": "WORKER ARCHITECTURE: cmd_infer and cmd_train are orchestrators that compose storage layer (fetch/put/list_uris) + segmenters (process_video) + reporting (emit_indexer_report) + training harness subprocess. Both operations use a local scratch directory (--scratch or tempdir) as the working directory; config.output.output_dir is overridden to scratch inside cmd_infer to keep output_dir semantics local-scoped. STORAGE LAYER (backend/storage/): URI-driven, lazy-loading cloud SDKs. fetch() is identity passthrough for local/bare paths, downloads for s3/gcs, mount-read for gdrive. put() copies local file to remote URI. list_uris() yields all objects under prefix (used by cmd_train to discover corpus labels/videos). BUCKET LAYOUT: Prefix-based on storage backend. Infer writes outputs/{video_id}/{intervals.csv,report.json}. Train writes weights/{version}/{weights.json,manifest.json}. Per-video keying is via MD5 (compute_video_id) in infer; training is version-keyed (--version arg). LOCAL BACKEND: No secrets, bare filesystem paths or local:// URIs. S3/GCS/GDrive: Credentials from environment chains (boto3 default, Application Default Credentials, Google Drive for Desktop mount). Config carries only endpoints/regions/paths. DATABASE: Not pushed to bucket — SQLite3 sports_indexer.db stays in scratch/output_dir only. WAL mode; auto-detects if FS doesn't support (networked paths). DEPENDENCIES: obsreport (soft) for per-video telemetry; training.gen0.harness (imported only in cmd_train subprocess call, not at module load). IDEMPOTENCE: video_id (MD5) is the join key; infer is idempotent on outputs/{video_id}/. Training is version-keyed; weights/{version}/ is the identity."
354	      },

### 116. assistant (2026-06-20T17:11:13.195Z)

# Per-video workspace — one folder per video, local⇄cloud identical

> **Status:** `DRAFT` — `in progress` (owner-requested 2026-06-20) · **Owner:** Avi · **Created:** 2026-06-20 · **Last updated:** 2026-06-20
> **Lifecycle:** `DRAFT → IN PROGRESS → DONE → archived` (move to `docs/archives/` when DONE)
> **Tracking anchors:** §7 progress tracker is the source of truth; pointer in memory `current-state`.
> **Relation to existing docs:** peer-of `DECOUPLED_COMPUTE` (reinforces the storage/compute seam) and the appliance track; extends `file-placement-conventions`.
> **Honesty note:** claims tagged `[verified]` (checked vs code by the mapping workflow), `[design]` (proposed), `[researched]`.

---

## 0. TL;DR
Today a single video's artifacts are **scattered** — `output/<family>/*.csv`, `output/chunks_<provider>_handoff/`, `~/clips/<key>_frames/`, `<scratch>/outputs/<video_id>/`, reels in `output/`, logs to stdout, plus DB rows. The mapping workflow found **56 write sites across 4 subsystems** `[verified]`. Goal: **one folder per video, keyed by the MD5 `video_id`, holding everything about that video** (working video copy/pointer, frames, trajectory, candidates, intervals/report, reel, run log) **except its metadata, which stays in the DB**. The root is a **storage URI**, so a **local** deployment and a **cloud (bucket)** deployment use the *same relative layout* — only the root differs → easy to find, easy to repro, easy to clean. Single most important caveat: this is a **default-OFF, phased** convergence (legacy flat layout untouched until a Launch-Report flip) because the worker's bucket push prefix and the WSL/native fast-scratch paths are load-bearing.

## 1. Problem & goal
- **Why now:** approaching launch + the DECOUPLED/Cloud-Run serving track. The owner wants per-video repro parity across local and cloud ("everything about a video resides in one place; metadata in the DB"). Ties [[feedback-launch-quality-bar]] (serious observability) and the Cloud-Run subproject (ADR-012).
- **Good looks like:** given a `video_id`, a single `ls <root>/<video_id>/` shows *all* of its artifacts, identically whether `<root>` is a local dir or `gs://…`. Deleting that one folder fully cleans the video (DB row aside).
- **Read to get current:** the §4 reuse map; `backend/utils/hashing.py::compute_video_id`; `backend/storage/` (URI abstraction); `backend/worker/__main__.py`; `backend/pipeline/segmenters/trajectory_hybrid.py`.

## 2. Decisions locked
Defaults chosen to keep momentum; the ⛔-marked ones in §8 are still owner-confirmable.

| # | Decision | Source / date | Implication |
|---|----------|---------------|-------------|
| D1 | **Key = full MD5 `video_id`** for the folder name (`<root>/<video_id>/`). | owner ask, 2026-06-20 | Matches `videos.id` / the storage join key; collision-proof. Artifact *filenames* keep the existing `video_id[:12]` infix. |
| D2 | **Root is a storage URI**; local & cloud share ONE relative layout (only the root differs). | design, [[monetization-plan]] | Reuses `backend/storage/` dispatch (`local://`/`gcs://`/`s3://`/`gdrive://`). No per-deployment code. |
| D3 | **Two roots:** a *durable* workspace root (may be `gs://`) for artifacts to keep; a *fast local compute-scratch* mirror (always local) for frame caches/chunks. Compute writes locally, then **pushes durable artifacts** to the workspace. | design + back-compat | WSL/native frame extraction never writes to a cloud root (perf + correctness). |
| D4 | **DB stays metadata-only** + ONE additive nullable `videos.workspace_uri` (v6). `NULL` = legacy flat layout. | design | No artifact bytes in the DB; the pointer makes a video's folder discoverable. |
| D5 | **Default-OFF behind a flag** (`storage.single_folder_per_video`); converge in phases; flip via a **Launch Report**. | [[launch-report-convention]] | Zero regression risk during convergence; the deployed bucket layout only changes on the gated flip. |
| D6 | **NOT per-video (stay outside the folder):** the `weights/<version>/` bundle (version-keyed), the SQLite DB, the WSL/native `wasb_infer.py` staging copy (infra), and the training corpus (`labels/`,`videos/`) + eval fixtures (shared inputs). | [verified] mapping | The convention covers *per-video* artifacts only; corpus/eval/model paths are intentionally excluded. |

## 3. Foundation — the artifact map  `[verified]`
The mapping workflow (`wf_085a5118`, 5 agents) catalogued every per-video write site. Consolidated:

| Artifact | Today | Keyed by | Target subfolder |
|---|---|---|---|
| Working video copy (cloud fetch) / source | `scratch/in.mp4`, `~/clips/<key>.<ext>` | video_id | `source/` (copy) or `source/source.uri` (local pointer) |
| Trajectory CSV (wasb/predict/stub) | `output/<family>/<key>_*.csv` | video_id | `detector/` |
| WASB detector cache (`det_raw.jsonl`,`manifest.json`) | `<csv_dir>/<key>_wasb_wasbcache/` | video_id | `detector/<key>_wasbcache/` |
| Frame PNG cache | `~/clips/<key>_frames/`, `<scratch>/<key>_frames/` | video_id | `tmp/` (**local-only**) |
| Handoff clips (AI validation) | `output/chunks_<provider>_handoff/` **(hardcoded)** | video_id+idx | `tmp/` (**local-only**) |
| YOLO person chunks | `output/chunks_yolo/` **(hardcoded)** | video_id+idx | `tmp/` (**local-only**) |
| Gemini chunks / temp-trim | `output/chunks/<video_id>/`, `output/temp_trimmed_<video_id>.mp4` | video_id | `tmp/` (**local-only**) |
| `intervals.csv`, `report.json` (worker) | `scratch/outputs/<video_id>/` → `<out_uri>/outputs/<video_id>/` | video_id | `outputs/` |
| Highlight reel | `output/<basename>.mp4` | video_id (via job) | `reels/` |
| Per-segment compile clips | `tempdir/clip_<idx>.mp4` | seg idx | `tmp/` (**local-only**) |
| Obsreport JSON | `output/<video_id>_report.json` | video_id | `outputs/` |
| Run log | stdout (not persisted) | — | `logs/run.log` |
| Candidate/play segments, jobs, events | SQLite tables | video_id | **DB only** (metadata) |

Two genuinely **hardcoded** dirs to kill: `output/chunks_<provider>_handoff` and `output/chunks_yolo` (this subsumes the long-carried "de-hardcode `output`" item, `trajectory_hybrid.py:237,313`).

## 4. Design / architecture
**Target layout (per video):**
```
<workspace_root>/<video_id>/
  source/        # working copy (cloud-fetched) OR source.uri pointer (local)
  detector/      # trajectory CSV(s) + <key>_wasbcache/ (det_raw.jsonl, manifest.json)
  outputs/       # intervals.csv, report.json, <video_id>_report.json (obsreport)
  reels/         # compiled highlight mp4(s)
  logs/          # run.log (per-video processing log)
  tmp/           # LOCAL-ONLY transient: frame PNGs, handoff/yolo/gemini chunks, compile clips — never pushed; cache-hygiene drops this
```
**Outside the per-video folder** (D6): `weights/<version>/`, the SQLite DB, `wasb_infer.py` staging, `labels/`+`videos/` corpus, eval fixtures.

**Reuse map (what's new vs reused):**
- **NEW** `backend/utils/workspace.py` — pure path helper: `video_workspace(root, video_id, *, sub=None, name=None)` builds `<root>/<video_id>[/<sub>][/<name>]`; a `WorkspaceLayout` with `.source/.detector/.outputs/.reels/.logs/.tmp`; a `compute_scratch_root()` that returns the fast LOCAL mirror (D3). No I/O — just string/URI joins (works for `local`/`gcs`/`s3` URIs).
- **REUSE** `backend/storage/` (`parse_uri`/`fetch`/`put`/`list_uris`) for all durable read/write; `compute_video_id` for the key; the existing `keep_frames`/`min_free_gb` cache-hygiene; `tools/cleanup_caches.py` (retarget to `tmp/`).
- **Adopt** at the call sites in §7 — each currently builds its own `os.path.join("output", …)`; they switch to the helper, gated by `single_folder_per_video`.

**Config:** `storage.workspace_root` (URI; default empty), `storage.single_folder_per_video` (bool, default **False**), `storage.workspace_prefix` (default `"workspaces"` for the cloud namespace). Root precedence: `storage.workspace_root` → `storage.output_root` → `output.output_dir`.

## 6. Honest limits — what this does NOT do
- A green suite proves the **helper + gated wiring** in unit tests; it does **NOT** prove the cloud bucket layout end-to-end until a real GPU run exercises the flag (deferred — the in-flight M2 run uses the *legacy* layout).
- It does **not** move corpus/eval/weights into the convention (D6) — those are shared, not per-video.
- It does **not** change the DB's role; metadata stays in SQLite.

## 7. Deliverables & progress tracker  ⟵ **source of truth**
Legend: ☐ Todo · ◐ In progress · ☑ Done · ⛔ Gated. One small PR per row, off `origin/master`, no stacking.

| ID | Deliverable | Depends on | Gated? | Status | PR |
|----|-------------|-----------|--------|--------|----|
| P0 | `backend/utils/workspace.py` helper + `storage.workspace_root`/`single_folder_per_video`/`workspace_prefix` config + unit tests (no wiring; no behavior change) | — | No | ◐ | this PR |
| P1 | v6 DB migration: nullable `videos.workspace_uri` + setter/getter | P0 | No | ☐ | — |
| P2 | Converge the **worker** (`cmd_infer`/`cmd_train` scratch + push) onto the workspace, flag-gated | P1 | No | ☐ | — |
| P3 | Converge **segmenter + detector** output dirs (kill `output/chunks_*` hardcodes; `detector/` + `tmp/`), flag-gated | P1 | No | ☐ | — |
| P4 | Converge **frame cache / WSL+native staging / reels / obsreport / run log** onto `tmp/`+`logs/`+`reels/`+`outputs/`, flag-gated | P3 | No | ☐ | — |
| P5 | **Launch Report** + flip default ON + retarget `tools/cleanup_caches.py` + docs | P2,P3,P4 | ⛔ owner | ☐ | — |
| P6 | Validate the flag on a real GPU run (local + a `gcs://` workspace) — repro parity | P5 | ⛔ owner GPU | ☐ | — |

## 8. Open questions — owner *(blocking-gates vs nice-to-knows)*
**Proposed defaults applied (override if you disagree):**
1. **Source video: copy or pointer?** → *default:* cloud-fetched copy lands in `source/`; a local source is referenced by a `source/source.uri` pointer (no duplication of large files). ⛔ confirm.
2. **Cloud root namespace?** → *default:* `gs://<bucket>/<workspace_prefix>/<video_id>/…` with `workspace_prefix="workspaces"` (keeps per-video folders tidy beside `videos/`,`labels/`,`weights/`; the legacy `outputs/<video_id>/` stays until P5). ⛔ confirm (affects the deployed bucket contract).
3. **Push transient chunks/frames to cloud?** → *default:* **No** — `tmp/` is local-only (huge + ephemeral). nice-to-know.
4. **Migration of existing artifacts?** → *default:* **forward-only** (legacy flat layout left in place; optional one-shot migrator later). nice-to-know.

## 9. Definition of done
- [ ] Helper + config land with tests (P0).
- [ ] `videos.workspace_uri` migration (P1).
- [ ] All §3 per-video write sites route through the helper under the flag (P2–P4); the two hardcoded `output/chunks_*` dirs are gone.
- [ ] Launch Report shows no regression; flag flipped ON; cleanup retargeted (P5).
- [ ] One real GPU run proves a `<video_id>/` folder is complete + identical local vs `gcs://` (P6).
- [ ] → flip Status DONE, archive.

### 122. assistant (2026-06-20T17:12:21.504Z)

"""Per-video workspace path helper — one folder per video, local⇄cloud identical.

See ``docs/PER_VIDEO_WORKSPACE.md``. The goal: every per-video artifact lives under
ONE folder keyed by the MD5 ``video_id`` (``backend/utils/hashing.py::compute_video_id``):

    <root>/[<prefix>/]<video_id>/{source,detector,outputs,reels,logs,tmp}/...

The ``root`` is a **storage URI** (``local`` path / ``gcs://`` / ``s3://`` / ``gdrive://``), so a
LOCAL deployment and a CLOUD (bucket) deployment share the *same relative layout* — only the root
differs. This module is **pure** (string/URI joins, no I/O) so it is trivially testable and works
for every backend; the storage layer (``backend/storage/``) does the actual read/write.

Two roots by design (D3): a **durable** workspace root (may be ``gcs://``) for artifacts worth
keeping, and a **fast local compute-scratch** mirror (always local) for frame caches / extracted
chunks that must never be written to a cloud root. ``tmp/`` is the local-only bucket.

This is Phase 0: the helper + config fields only — NOT yet wired into write sites (that converges
in P2–P4, flag-gated by ``storage.single_folder_per_video``). No behaviour changes on import.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
from dataclasses import dataclass

# Per-video subfolders. ``tmp`` is LOCAL-ONLY (frame PNGs, handoff/yolo/gemini chunks, compile
# clips) — huge + ephemeral, never pushed to a cloud workspace; cache-hygiene drops it.
SUBDIRS = ("source", "detector", "outputs", "reels", "logs", "tmp")
LOCAL_ONLY_SUBDIRS = ("tmp",)


def join_uri(root: str, *parts: str) -> str:
    """Join path components onto a storage root with forward slashes.

    Matches how the codebase already composes storage URIs (e.g. ``f"{prefix}/weights/..."`` in
    ``backend/worker``). Works for ``gcs://bucket``, ``s3://bucket``, ``local:///abs`` and bare
    paths alike; empty/blank parts are skipped so ``join_uri(root, "", "x")`` == ``join_uri(root,
    "x")``. Forward slashes are valid path separators in Python on Windows too.
    """
    base = root.rstrip("/")
    tail = "/".join(p.strip("/") for p in parts if p and p.strip("/"))
    return f"{base}/{tail}" if tail else base


def resolve_workspace_root(workspace_root: str, output_root: str, output_dir: str) -> str:
    """Durable workspace root, by precedence: ``storage.workspace_root`` →
    ``storage.output_root`` → ``output.output_dir``. The first non-empty wins (non-breaking)."""
    return (workspace_root or "").strip() or (output_root or "").strip() or (output_dir or "output")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VideoWorkspace:
    """The single folder for one video. Pure path math; build with :func:`for_video`.

    ``base`` = ``<root>/[<prefix>/]<video_id>``. Subfolder accessors return URIs/paths under it.
    """

    root: str
    video_id: str
    prefix: str = "workspaces"

    @property
    def base(self) -> str:
        if self.prefix:
            return join_uri(self.root, self.prefix, self.video_id)
        return join_uri(self.root, self.video_id)

    def path(self, sub: str = "", name: str = "") -> str:
        """A path under this video's folder: ``base[/sub][/name]``."""
        return join_uri(self.base, sub, name)

    @property
    def source(self) -> str:
        return self.path("source")

    @property
    def detector(self) -> str:
        return self.path("detector")

    @property
    def outputs(self) -> str:
        return self.path("outputs")

    @property
    def reels(self) -> str:
        return self.path("reels")

    @property
    def logs(self) -> str:
        return self.path("logs")

    @property
    def tmp(self) -> str:
        return self.path("tmp")


def for_video(root: str, video_id: str, prefix: str = "workspaces") -> VideoWorkspace:
    """Build the :class:`VideoWorkspace` for ``video_id`` under ``root`` (a storage URI)."""
    if not video_id:
        raise ValueError("video_id is required to build a per-video workspace")
    return VideoWorkspace(root=root, video_id=video_id, prefix=prefix)


def local_scratch_base(scratch_root: str, video_id: str) -> str:
    """The fast LOCAL compute-scratch mirror for a video (always native FS, never a cloud root).

    Used for frame caches / extracted chunks (the ``tmp/`` bucket) during compute, before durable
    artifacts are pushed to the workspace. Uses ``os.path.join`` (OS-native separators) since this
    is always a local filesystem path.
    """
    if not video_id:
        raise ValueError("video_id is required for a local scratch path")
    return os.path.join(scratch_root or os.getcwd(), video_id, "tmp")

### 125. assistant (2026-06-20T17:12:48.331Z)

"""Unit tests for the per-video workspace helper (docs/PER_VIDEO_WORKSPACE.md, Phase 0).

Pure path math — no I/O. The key property under test is **local⇄cloud parity**: the same
relative layout under different roots.
"""
import os

import pytest

from backend.utils.workspace import (
    LOCAL_ONLY_SUBDIRS,
    SUBDIRS,
    VideoWorkspace,
    for_video,
    join_uri,
    local_scratch_base,
    resolve_workspace_root,
)

VID = "27f1c707bbfb0a1b2c3d4e5f60718293"  # MD5-shaped video_id


class TestJoinUri:
    def test_basic_join(self):
        assert join_uri("gs://bucket", "workspaces", VID) == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}"

    def test_strips_trailing_and_inner_slashes(self):
        assert join_uri("gs://bucket/", "/a/", "/b/") == "gs://bucket/a/b"

    def test_skips_blank_parts(self):
        assert join_uri("root", "", "x", "   ", "y") == "root/x/y"
        assert join_uri("root") == "root"
        assert join_uri("root", "") == "root"

    def test_works_for_local_and_s3_and_bare(self):
        assert join_uri("local:///srv/data", VID) == f"local:///srv/data/{VID}"
        assert join_uri("s3://b", "k") == "s3://b/k"
        assert join_uri("output", VID, "outputs") == f"output/{VID}/outputs"


class TestResolveWorkspaceRoot:
    def test_precedence_workspace_first(self):
        assert resolve_workspace_root("gs://ws", "gs://out", "output") == "gs://ws"

    def test_falls_back_to_output_root(self):
        assert resolve_workspace_root("", "gs://out", "output") == "gs://out"

    def test_falls_back_to_output_dir(self):
        assert resolve_workspace_root("", "", "myout") == "myout"

    def test_default_when_all_empty(self):
        assert resolve_workspace_root("", "", "") == "output"

    def test_whitespace_is_ignored(self):
        assert resolve_workspace_root("   ", "gs://out", "output") == "gs://out"


class TestVideoWorkspace:
    def test_base_with_prefix(self):
        ws = for_video("gs://bucket", VID)
        assert ws.base == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}"

    def test_base_without_prefix(self):
        ws = for_video("gs://bucket", VID, prefix="")
        assert ws.base == f"gs://bucket/{VID}"

    def test_subdir_accessors(self):
        ws = for_video("gs://bucket", VID)
        assert ws.source == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/source"
        assert ws.detector == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/detector"
        assert ws.outputs == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/outputs"
        assert ws.reels == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/reels"
        assert ws.logs == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/logs"
        assert ws.tmp == f"gs://bucket/workspaces/{VID}/tmp"

    def test_path_with_name(self):
        ws = for_video("output", VID, prefix="")
        assert ws.path("detector", f"clip__{VID[:12]}_wasb.csv") == (
            f"output/{VID}/detector/clip__{VID[:12]}_wasb.csv"
        )

    def test_all_subdirs_are_under_base(self):
        ws = for_video("gs://b", VID)
        for sub in SUBDIRS:
            assert ws.path(sub).startswith(ws.base + "/")

    def test_for_video_requires_video_id(self):
        with pytest.raises(ValueError):
            for_video("gs://b", "")

    def test_frozen_dataclass(self):
        ws = VideoWorkspace(root="r", video_id=VID)
        with pytest.raises(Exception):
            ws.root = "other"  # type: ignore[misc]


class TestLocalCloudParity:
    """The headline property: identical RELATIVE layout under any root."""

    def test_same_relative_layout_across_roots(self):
        local = for_video("/srv/khelsutra/data", VID, prefix="workspaces")
        cloud = for_video("gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus", VID, prefix="workspaces")
        for sub in SUBDIRS:
            rel_local = local.path(sub)[len("/srv/khelsutra/data"):]
            rel_cloud = cloud.path(sub)[len("gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus"):]
            assert rel_local == rel_cloud == f"/workspaces/{VID}/{sub}"


class TestLocalScratch:
    def test_uses_os_native_join(self):
        got = local_scratch_base(os.path.join("C:", "scratch"), VID)
        assert got == os.path.join("C:", "scratch", VID, "tmp")

    def test_requires_video_id(self):
        with pytest.raises(ValueError):
            local_scratch_base("/scratch", "")


def test_tmp_is_local_only():
    assert "tmp" in LOCAL_ONLY_SUBDIRS
    assert "outputs" not in LOCAL_ONLY_SUBDIRS

The convention: `<root>/[workspaces/]<video_id>/{source,detector,outputs,reels,logs,tmp}`, root = a storage URI so **local and cloud are identical**; `tmp/` (frames/chunks) is local-only; weights/DB/corpus stay outside. Two decisions affect the *deployed contract* — I've defaulted them but flag for confirmation:
1. **Cloud namespace:** `gs://<bucket>/workspaces/<video_id>/…` (keeps per-video folders tidy beside `videos/`,`weights/`; legacy `outputs/<video_id>/` untouched until the gated flip).
2. **Source video:** cloud-fetched copy in `source/`; local source referenced by a pointer (no duplicating large files).

### 148. assistant (2026-06-20T17:20:11.702Z)

---
name: gcp-vm-always-delete
description: Cost rule — DELETE rally GPU VMs when a run finishes, never just STOP (a stopped VM still bills its disk). Audit for orphaned disks.
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Owner directive (2026-06-20): when a rented GPU VM's run is done, **DELETE it — don't just STOP it.**

**Why:** a STOPPED GCP VM halts *compute* billing but **still bills for the persistent disk** (pd-ssd ≈ $0.17/GB/mo → a 200 GB boot disk ≈ **$34/mo**) — a silent cost. **DELETE** removes the VM and (boot disk `autoDelete=true` by default) its disk → **$0**. Verified 2026-06-20: `rally-gpu`'s boot disk has `autoDelete=True`, so `instances delete` reclaims it; no orphan.

**How to apply:**
- Tear down with **`delete`**: `bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` (the teardown default is now `delete`, not `stop` — the old `stop` default was the cost trap).
- **Cross-region VM:** set the zone — `RALLY_GCP_ZONE=<zone> bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` (teardown defaults to `asia-south1-a`; the 2026-06-20 M2 VM was in `asia-southeast1-a`).
- **Audit for orphans after deleting:** `gcloud compute instances list` (empty) AND `gcloud compute disks list` (no unattached disk — an orphaned disk bills even with no VM). A manually-attached data/local-SSD disk may need explicit removal even when the VM is gone.
- A **RUNNING** VM that's actively doing work is fine to leave up until the job completes — then delete **immediately**; never leave a GPU idle. The `$100` budget guard (`rally-100usd-guard`) is a backstop, not the primary control.

**Also billing (not GCP, not auto-deletable):** the DO droplet `claude-do-rally-test` (blr1, CPU `s-2vcpu-4gb`, ≈ $24/mo) is a **shared testbed** (another session's engine + the site twin) → owner-gated cleanup, not mine to delete. [[gcp-ops-agent-enable]] [[monetization-plan]]

## Free Trial Credit Overview

DigitalOcean offers all new users $200 in free credit with a 60-day validity period. This promotional credit lets you test-drive the entire DigitalOcean platform — Droplets, Kubernetes, managed databases, App Platform, GPU Droplets, and more.

## GPU Droplets Eligibility

All products eligible: Unlike some cloud providers that restrict free credits to specific services, DigitalOcean credits work on everything. This means GPU Droplets are included and can be tested with your $200 credit.

## Eligibility Requirements

The credit applies to new accounts only, and you'll need a valid credit card or PayPal to verify. Add a credit card or link your PayPal account. You won't be charged until you exceed the $200 credit. A temporary hold of ~$5 may appear on credit cards for verification.

## Additional Offers

If you're a student, there's also a separate GitHub Student Developer Pack deal that gives $200 valid for a full year.

If you are a newer DigitalOcean customer, DigitalOcean may charge your team's primary payment method partway through the monthly billing cycle when you create GPU Droplets, but your total cost is not increased.

This note appears in DigitalOcean's official Droplet Pricing documentation and is also mentioned in their GPU Droplet creation guide. The key points are:

1. This applies specifically to **newer customers**
2. The charge is applied to your **primary payment method**
3. It happens **partway through the monthly billing cycle** when GPU Droplets are created
4. **The total cost is not increased** - it's just timing adjustment for billing purposes

The search results didn't return specific information about credit eligibility restrictions, but the documentation confirms this is standard billing behavior for newer DigitalOcean customers using GPU Droplets.

### 181. assistant (2026-06-20T17:27:21.427Z)

GPU just pegged 100% on the L4 — M2 inference is actively crunching video 1 now (the bursty 0→100% pattern is the decode-bound reality on a 4-vCPU box). Still progressing. Now, your DO/Paperspace question — researched against DigitalOcean's own docs:

## Short answer: there's no *clean* "free-credits GPU" path right now — on either DO Droplets or Paperspace

**DO GPU Droplets — credits technically apply, but not cleanly for a new account like ours:**
- DO's own pricing page says the $200 promo credit *does* cover GPU Droplets ("test-drive GPU Droplets… without paying out of pocket"). So in principle, **yes**.
- **But** DO's billing + GPU-create docs repeat an anti-abuse carve-out: *"If you are a newer DigitalOcean customer, we may charge your team's primary payment method partway through the monthly billing cycle when you create GPU Droplets."* Translation: as a newer account, DO **exercises your real card** when you spin up GPU — you can't treat it as "credits only, card never touched."
- **And the bigger blocker:** our account literally can't create GPU Droplets yet — I verified earlier that `/v2/sizes` returns **0 GPU sizes**. GPU Droplets are also **North-America-only** (NYC2/TOR1/ATL1/RIC1/AMS3) and some (AMD) explicitly need "contact support to request access." So it needs console verification / a support request before it's even an option.

**Paperspace (now "Gradient by DigitalOcean") — your instinct was exactly right:**
- The **free GPU tier requires a paid subscription** (a "G*" plan); the better GPUs (A100/H100) need the **$39/mo Growth** plan.
- Paperspace machines bill **separately** from DO Droplet credits — **the $200 DO credit does not fund Paperspace GPUs**. So it's not a free-credits path at all.

## Recommendation
1. **Don't chase free GPU on DO for this.** Even if you enable GPU Droplets (verify payment method in console → possibly a support ticket), a newer account gets its card charged for GPU, and there's no Asia region. The "$200 credits → free GPU training" premise behind **ADR-011 (DO=training)** doesn't really hold — I'd flag that as a finding worth amending.
2. **Keep training on GCP** — which *works today* (we're mid-run on an L4 for ~$1–2, quota granted, no subscription). Real money, but a couple dollars per run.
3. **Spend the $200 DO credits where they actually apply cleanly** — CPU droplets, the khelsutra site host, Spaces/storage, bandwidth (all credit-eligible, no card-exercise gotcha).
4. If you *do* want DO GPU specifically: cheapest single-GPU is ~$0.76–$2.50/hr (RTX 4000/6000 Ada, L40S), so a 1–2 hr run is a few dollars to the card regardless of credits — only worth enabling if you want DO as the stack; otherwise GCP is less friction.

I can **amend ADR-011** to record this (GCP is the practical training path; DO credits → CPU/site/storage, not GPU) — but that's a strategy call, so I'll leave it to you to greenlight rather than bank it unilaterally.

**Sources:** [DO GPU Droplets pricing](https://www.digitalocean.com/pricing/gpu-droplets) · [DO Droplet pricing docs (newer-customer GPU charge)](https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/droplets/details/pricing/) · [How to Create GPU Droplets](https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/droplets/how-to/gpu/create/) · [Paperspace free instances (subscription-gated)](https://github.com/Paperspace/Docs/blob/master/instances/instance-types/free-instances.md) · [Paperspace pricing / $39 Growth](https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/paperspace/pricing/)

Segment clearing is now an explicit destroy-AFTER-confirm via `segment_watermarks`/`prune_segments` (or `clear_video_data`). `_get_connection` MUST still re-issue `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` per connection (CASCADE/SET NULL still apply on an explicit delete).

11	**$0/mo**, saving GCP credits; **Beta: GCP control plane + your box as pull-worker** (Cloud Run +
12	GCS + Firebase Auth, credits-covered), keeping the edge where it's free.

### 191. user (2026-06-20T17:32:15.350Z)

1	# Setting up a new machine — zero → training & inference
2	
3	**The canonical "spin up a box and make the models ready" runbook.** Copy-pasteable; followable by
4	any human. DigitalOcean is the worked example (GCP/AWS/Paperspace are analogous — only the provision
5	step differs).
6	
7	> **Last verified:** 2026-06-20 on a DigitalOcean Droplet (Ubuntu 24.04, 2 vCPU / 3.8 GB, **no GPU**).
8	> Bootstrap succeeded (torch 2.12.1+cpu); full mocked suite **922 passed / 11 skipped**; a **secret-free**
9	> CPU inference (no GPU, no API key) detected **2 rallies** and persisted them to SQLite.
10	>
11	> ⚠ Assumes the DECOUPLED_COMPUTE PRs are merged: the CPU-mock detector seam, `deploy/digitalocean/bootstrap.sh`,
12	> the storage layer, and the worker dispatcher (#251). The **bucket** steps (§7) are config-driven and
13	> **verified against real DO Spaces + GCS** — see the appendix.
14	
15	## What you'll have at the end
16	
17	A machine that can: run the test suite (green), run **secret-free CPU inference** (rallies, no GPU/keys),
18	run **real Gemini inference** (with a key), and run **Gen-0 model training** — plus pointers to the GPU /
19	native-detector path and bucket-driven train/infer.
20	
21	---
22	
23	## 1. Credentials to create once (least-privilege)
24	
25	| # | Credential | Where | Needed for |
26	|---|---|---|---|
27	| 1 | **SSH keypair** (public key added to the provider) | `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -f ~/.ssh/rally` → paste `.pub` into DO → Settings → Security | logging into the box |
28	| 2 | **DigitalOcean API token** (read+write) | DO → API → Tokens | `doctl` provisioning (optional — you can click-create) |
29	| 3 | **GitHub repo read access** | a read-only **deploy key** *or* a fine-grained **PAT** (`Contents: Read`) | `git clone` on the box (or use the `scp` path in §3) |
30	| 4 | **`GEMINI_API_KEY`** | Google AI Studio / Vertex | **real** Gemini inference only (§6b). The secret-free path (§6a) needs none. **Rotate before sharing.** |
31	| 5 | **Bucket creds** — DO Spaces key/secret, or GCS SA JSON, or Drive SA JSON + folder | provider console | bucket-driven train/infer (§7, *pending PR-B*) |
32	| 6 | **GPU box** (Paperspace free M4000 / GPU Droplet) | provider console | real WASB/TrackNet detectors + faster runs (§8) |
33	
34	**Secret hygiene:** never put a secret in the repo, a PR, or `config.json`. Set them as env vars on the box
35	(or a git-ignored `.env`). Scope each as narrowly as the UI allows and rotate/revoke when done.
36	
37	---
38	
39	## 2. Provision the machine
40	
41	> **⚠ Stack split by purpose (2026-06-20, ADR-011, refines ADR-010):**
42	> **TRAINING / the DECOUPLED_COMPUTE core runs on DigitalOcean** — use the **$200 credits**; spin a **DO
43	> GPU droplet** (`tor1`/`nyc2`, North-America-only — there is no India/Singapore DO GPU) + **co-located DO
44	> Spaces** (`nyc3`). Follow the DO steps below + `deploy/digitalocean/*` (the scripts are provider-agnostic).
45	> **The SERVING path to real customers runs on GCP** (Cloud Run GPU scale-to-zero = per-customer billing —
46	> M3/M4, monetization track); the GCS bucket `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus` + SA + `$100` budget
47	> (`deploy/gcp/README.md`) are retained for that. The native WASB runner (`detector_impl="native"`) runs the
48	> same on either Linux GPU box.
49	
50	- **CPU box** (suite + secret-free stub inference + Gen-0 training plumbing): cheapest **Ubuntu 24.04**
51	  Droplet, **2 vCPU / 4 GB**, region nearest you, **attach the SSH key** (#1). ~$0.06/hr — destroy when done.
52	- **GPU box** (real WASB/TrackNet detectors → real trajectory extraction + training): a **DO GPU Droplet**.
53	  ⚠ You **cannot add a GPU to a CPU Droplet** — it's a separate machine type, in **GPU regions**
54	  (`nyc2`/`tor1`/`atl1`, **not** `blr1`), with **dual local NVMe**. **RTX 4000 Ada (20 GB, ~$0.76/hr)** is
55	  plenty for WASB + Gen-0; the `gpu-*-base` image preinstalls the NVIDIA driver. **Co-locate the Space in the
56	  GPU box's region** for cheap corpus-pull.
57	
58	CLI provision (after `doctl auth init` with token #2 — **verify size/image slugs first** via `doctl compute size list`):
59	```bash
60	# CPU box:
61	doctl compute droplet create rally-box --image ubuntu-24-04-x64 --size s-2vcpu-4gb \
62	  --region blr1 --ssh-keys <your-key-fp> --wait
63	# GPU box (RTX 4000 Ada):
64	doctl compute droplet create rally-gpu --image gpu-h100x1-base --size gpu-4000adax1-20gb \
65	  --region tor1 --ssh-keys <your-key-fp> --wait
66	doctl compute droplet list   # note the IP -> ssh -i ~/.ssh/rally root@<IP>

### 207. assistant (2026-06-20T17:36:06.393Z)

---
name: compute-stack-gcp-only
description: Standing decision (ADR-013, 2026-06-20) — GCP is the SOLE compute stack (training + serving); DigitalOcean is DROPPED for compute. Don't relitigate.
metadata:
  type: project
---

**Owner decision, 2026-06-20 (ADR-013):** "drop DO and document it… record it and bake it for all future work." → **GCP is the sole compute stack for BOTH training and serving. DigitalOcean is dropped for compute.** This supersedes the ADR-011 "DO=training, GCP=serving" split.

**Why (researched):** DO GPU Droplets aren't enabled on the account (`/v2/sizes` = 0 GPU sizes), are North-America-only, and DO charges **newer customers' cards** for GPU (anti-abuse) → **not cleanly funded by the $200 credit**. Paperspace (DO Gradient) free GPU is **subscription-gated** ($39/mo Growth) and bills separately from DO credits. GCP works today (quota granted, bucket+SA+$100 budget, L4 ~$1–2/run, one portable PyTorch+CUDA stack local↔VM↔Cloud Run).

**How to apply (the bake):**
- All compute provisioning → **`deploy/gcp/`** only (GPU VM now; Cloud Run+GPU for serving per ADR-012 + [[current-state]] "Cloud Run subproject"). **Do NOT re-evaluate DO/Paperspace for compute without a new ADR.**
- The `$200` DO credits, if used, go ONLY to non-compute DO bills cleanly (CPU droplet / site host / Spaces storage / bandwidth) — **never GPU**.
- `deploy/digitalocean/` scripts (`bootstrap.sh`/`mount_scratch.sh`/`gpu_setup.sh`) are KEPT — they're **provider-agnostic** Linux-GPU setup reused by the GCP runbook; only DO *provisioning* (doctl, Spaces) is retired. Optional later: move them to `deploy/common/`.
- Storage stays bucket-abstracted (`gcs://` primary); the `s3` backend still speaks DO Spaces if ever needed for *storage* only.
- The shared DO droplet `claude-do-rally-test` (other session's testbed) + retiring the DO API token = owner-coordinated, separate from this. See [[gcp-vm-always-delete]] [[gcp-ops-agent-enable]] [[monetization-plan]].

42	## Run results
43	- **M3.5 parity** (`wasb_infer.py --device cuda`, smoke clip, limit 1200): produced
44	  `outputs/parity/native_smoke_l4.csv` (1200 frames). vs the 2070 WSL baseline: **511/1195 identical,
45	  684 differ sub-pixel** (e.g. 968.01→967.87 px), visible 682 vs 684. → parity within ~tenths-of-a-px
46	  cross-GPU; **not** byte-exact across GPUs.
47	- **M1 decoupled infer** (`worker infer detector_impl=native`, smoke clip, full 5393 windows on GPU):
48	  **22 rallies** → `outputs/<md5>/intervals.csv` + `report.json` pushed.
49	- **M2 real-trajectory train** (`worker train --trajectory-source detector`, mahadevpura-1 + GX030094):
50	  **real extraction VALIDATED in-flight** — the native runner extracted **16 000+/21 195 windows of
51	  GX030094 on the L4 GPU** before the clean-slate teardown (overwhelming evidence the real-trajectory
52	  train path works e2e). The full train → versioned weights (`weights/0.1.0-l4/`) is a **next-session
53	  item**: re-port is one-shot via the fixed `gpu_setup.sh`; **set `indexing.wasb.stream_video=false`
54	  for the long training videos** (disk extraction keeps the GPU fed — finding #6).
55	
56	## Findings / gotchas (all fixed or noted)
57	1. **conda Terms-of-Service gate** — modern conda blocks non-interactive `conda create`
58	   (`CondaToSNonInteractiveError`). **Fixed in `gpu_setup.sh`** (`conda tos accept …`).
59	2. **WASB-SBDT `requirements.txt` is incomplete** — `src/` imports `hydra`, `pandas`, etc. it omits.
60	   **Fixed in `gpu_setup.sh`** (explicit `hydra-core omegaconf pandas opencv-python-headless pillow scipy
61	   tqdm pyyaml matplotlib numpy<1.24`).
62	3. **T4 capacity stockout** in `asia-south1-a` → **L4 in `-c`** landed (g2-standard-4; L4 is built into g2,
63	   no `--accelerator` flag). Try L4/other zones when T4 is unavailable.
64	4. **Spot does NOT bypass `GPUS_ALL_REGIONS`** (tested) — both on-demand + Spot need the quota.
65	5. **SSH-foreground long commands die on a dropped connection** — run them `nohup`'d on the box + poll the
66	   log. (Runbook updated.)
67	6. **Streaming vs disk for extraction** — streaming (the native default) is the win for SHORT clips (skips
68	   the 46k-PNG write), but for a LONG training video its single-process decode **starves the GPU**; the
69	   disk path (`stream_video=false`, parallel `num_workers`) keeps the GPU fed → **prefer disk extraction
70	   for long training videos**. (Follow-up: a per-path default / train-uses-disk knob.)
71	7. **Observability** needs THREE things, all initially missing on the bare Gemini project: (a) the
72	   **Cloud Logging + Monitoring APIs ENABLED** (`gcloud services enable logging… monitoring…`) — this was
73	   the actual empty-graphs cause: the agent installs + runs but its exports get
74	   `PermissionDenied/SERVICE_DISABLED` until the APIs are on; (b) the SA's **monitoring.metricWriter +
75	   logging.logWriter** roles; (c) the **Ops Agent** itself (direct install — the console OS-policy install
76	   is blocked on DLVM images; that console error is a red herring). All done + the agent restarted →
77	   exports clean (0 errors). Runbook §3b has the full sequence (best baked into the VM startup-script).
78	
79	## Cost
80	L4 (g2-standard-4) on-demand ≈ **$0.70–0.85/hr**; the validation run is ~1–1.5 hr ⇒ **~$1**. The `$100`
81	budget alert is the backstop. **STOP/DELETE the VM when idle** — `deploy/gcp/teardown.sh`.
82	
83	## Teardown
84	`bash deploy/gcp/teardown.sh delete` (frees the GPU; keeps the bucket). The bucket/SA/budget are retained
85	for the GCP serving path (ADR-011).
86	
87	**Session-end state (2026-06-20):** the **L4 VM was DELETED** (clean slate, **$0 ongoing compute**) — the
88	bucket, SA (`rally-worker`), and `$100` budget are retained. The next session **re-ports one-shot** via the

90	(`dop_v1_…`, accidentally written as a filename) was removed from the repo root → **rotate that DO token**.
91

### 253. user (2026-06-21T00:42:46.160Z)

1	# Decoupled Compute — Cloud-Native GPU for Train + Inference
2	
3	**Status:** 🚧 ACTIVE — **scope narrowed (ADR-011): DoD = M0–M2 + M3.5; M3/M4 deferred to the monetization track.** Seams + M3.5 native runner SHIPPED + validated (PRs #246–#259); **remaining = ONE real GPU run on DigitalOcean** (M1/M2/M3.5). *Was "no code yet" at proposal time — long since false.*
4	**Date:** 2026-06-20 · **Owner:** avidullu
5	**Lifecycle:** *Critical active project.* When delivered (after the DO GPU run), **move this whole directory to `docs/archives/past_projects/`** (keeps the strategy tree clean).
6	
7	> **What this directory is.** A single, unified place that answers one question: *can we lift the
8	> rally-segmentation pipeline off the owner's local Windows/WSL box and run it on a **rented,
9	> credit-budgeted, cloud-native GPU** — driven by an API key — that does **both** (a) **retrain** the
10	> rally model from a **bucket of videos** and (b) run **inference on new uploads** — and **bill each
11	> video / each training run exactly**, including for a non-owner user?* Short answer: the *design*
12	> already exists across our strategy docs; the *build* (a container/VM image, a real bucket, a chosen
13	> provider, a cost ledger) does not. This directory inventories what's planned, maps the real compute,
14	> compares platforms, and lays out a cheapest-first build & test plan that fits the **$200** budget.
15	
16	---
17	
18	## North Star
19	
20	A **decoupled** pipeline where the **control plane** (a thin API that accepts jobs and reports status)
21	is separated from the **data/compute plane** (the heavy bytes + GPU work), which runs in a **rented
22	cloud GPU** reading from and writing to an **object-store bucket**. Two verbs:
23	
24	- **`train`** — consume a bucket of videos + labels → produce versioned model weights back to the bucket.
25	- **`infer`** — a new uploaded video → rally segments + highlight reel + an eval report back to the bucket.
26	
27	…with **exact per-video (inference) and per-run (training) cost attribution** so the cost can be
28	charged to whoever ran the job.
29	
30	---
31	
32	## TL;DR (read this, then dive into the numbered docs)
33	
34	1. **Yes — decoupling is already designed, in depth.** [`PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md`](../PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md)
35	   is a control-plane/data-plane split with a `ComputeBackend` registry (`local | hosted | byo_worker`)
36	   and a `StorageBackend` registry (S3/GCS/Azure via `fsspec`); [`HOSTING_PLAN.md`](../HOSTING_PLAN.md)
37	   already names *"GPU box = first `byo_worker`"* with GCS buckets + signed-URL uploads; §5A specifies
38	   **training on a rented GPU** via `ms-swift`. → **[01](01-EXISTING-PLANS-AND-GAP.md)**.
39	2. **What's missing is everything that turns those seams into the North Star:** a container/VM image,
40	   a **chosen** provider + an **API-key/provisioning** flow, a **real bucket** (today it's OneDrive
41	   auto-sync — a proto-bucket), a **download-to-local** shim, path de-hardcoding, a **bucket-triggered
42	   retrain**, a **remote inference endpoint**, and **cost metering**. → **[01](01-EXISTING-PLANS-AND-GAP.md)**, **[02](02-COMPUTE-MAP-AND-CONTRACT.md)**.
43	3. **$200 is comfortable — for the *right* scope.** It easily covers the **inference pipeline + Gen-0
44	   head training** (numpy-class, trains in minutes on a small GPU) decoupled onto one cheap GPU. It does
45	   **NOT** cover the **Gen-2 VLM fine-tune** (ms-swift/QLoRA, multi-GPU, *"the largest open risk,
46	   currently unquantified"*) — that stays explicitly **out of scope** for this budget. → **[03](03-PLATFORM-OPTIONS.md)**, **[06](06-RISKS-AND-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md)**.
47	4. **Docker is optional.** Serverless/Cloud Run paths need an image; a **DO Droplet** or **SkyPilot**
48	   path can run the repo in a plain `venv`/`conda` with no container. We recommend a container *where you
49	   go serverless* and a VM/SkyPilot path otherwise. → **[03](03-PLATFORM-OPTIONS.md)**.
50	5. **Per-video billing falls out of a serverless per-invocation metering model + a cost ledger on the
51	   already-shipped `jobs` table.** → **[05](05-COST-ATTRIBUTION.md)**.
52	6. **Cheapest-first: M0 costs $0** — a CPU container/venv runs the **committed synthetic, video-free
53	   regression fixtures** ([`golden_fixtures.py`](../../backend/eval/golden_fixtures.py)) in CI, no GPU,
54	   no WSL. Only spend a dollar at M1. → **[04](04-BUILD-AND-TEST-PLAN.md)**.
55	7. **The WSL detectors run seamlessly off the Windows box — and we prove it on DigitalOcean.** The
56	   `DetectorRunner` ABC is already built for the swap (WSL plumbing is an isolated mixin; the `Frame,
57	   Visibility,X,Y` CSV is the parity contract); a DO GPU is Ubuntu+CUDA = your WSL conda env, **minus
58	   WSL**. A dedicated **M3.5 parity test on DO** is the guarantee, and it doubles as the
59	   `DESKTOP_APP_PLAN` P0 spike. → **[02](02-COMPUTE-MAP-AND-CONTRACT.md)**, **[04](04-BUILD-AND-TEST-PLAN.md)**, **[06](06-RISKS-AND-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md)**.
60	
61	---
62	
63	## The budget tension you must decide (see [03](03-PLATFORM-OPTIONS.md) + [06](06-RISKS-AND-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md))
64	
65	> **✅ RESOLVED (ADR-011, 2026-06-20):** split by purpose — **DigitalOcean = TRAINING + the decoupling
66	> core** (use the $200 credits; GPU `tor1`/`nyc2` + Spaces `nyc3`), **GCP = the SERVING path to real
67	> customers** (Cloud Run GPU scale-to-zero = native per-customer per-invocation billing = M3/M4, now in
68	> the monetization track). DECOUPLED_COMPUTE's DoD = M0–M2 + M3.5 on DO. The rest of this section is the
69	> original framing that led to that decision.
70	
71	You have **$200 in DigitalOcean**, and you're open to **GCS + a Cloud-Run-like** architecture. These pull
72	in different directions for the *side goal* (exact per-video billing):
73	
74	- **DigitalOcean** has the credits + **Spaces** (S3-compatible, **1 TiB egress included** — the best
75	  video-download deal), but **no true serverless GPU**, so per-video metering is coarser (you bill
76	  wall-clock on a started/stopped GPU Droplet).
77	- **GCP Cloud Run (GPU) / RunPod Serverless** give **scale-to-zero + exact per-invocation GPU-seconds**
78	  — which *is* your per-video bill — but the $200 isn't DO credit there (GCP's $300/90-day trial is the
79	  clean free tier; RunPod's "free credit" is small).
80	
81	**Recommended split:** prototype M0–M2 on **DO (use the credits) + DO Spaces**; stand up the M3 inference
82	*endpoint* where per-invocation metering is native (Cloud Run GPU / RunPod Serverless). Final call is
83	owner's — tracked in [06](06-RISKS-AND-OPEN-QUESTIONS.md).
84	
85	---

### 259. user (2026-06-21T00:45:30.859Z)

1	# 06 · Risks & open questions
2	
3	**Status:** PROPOSAL · **Date:** 2026-06-20
4	
5	> The technical plan is cheap and de-risked. The *gates* are mostly **scope, legal, and budget-framing** —
6	> plus a few staleness checks. This doc is the adversarial review (a completeness critic ran over the build
7	> plan) distilled into risks + the decisions only the owner can make.
8	
9	## 1. Risks (ranked)
10	
11	1. **WSL2 bridge is the load-bearing technical risk — but the codebase is already built to drop it.** Both
12	   high-accuracy detectors (WASB, TrackNet) run *only* via `wsl … conda activate` with hardcoded `~/models`
13	   paths, hardcoded `device='cuda'`, un-vendored repos, and **conflicting TF-vs-torch runtimes.** The
14	   Linux-native path is **plausible but unproven** — it has never been run, and shipping a model commercially
15	   is gated on weight-licensing (WASB-C3). *What's already in our favor:* the `DetectorRunner` ABC
16	   ([`detectors/base.py`](../../backend/pipeline/detectors/base.py)) was built *expressly* for this swap —
17	   WSL plumbing lives in a separate `WslCommandMixin` ("*a future Linux-native runner must not inherit WSL
18	   plumbing*"), the base preserves *"existing behavior 100%"* for Windows dev, and any runner emitting the
19	   same `Frame,Visibility,X,Y` CSV slots in verbatim. *Mitigation:* ship the torchvision/Gemini/Gen-0 path
20	   first (M0–M3); prove the native runner via the **M3.5 WSL→native parity test on DigitalOcean** (DO GPU =
21	   Ubuntu+CUDA = the native conda env, minus WSL; WASB's shipped badminton weights make a real trajectory
22	   testable today). **That parity test IS the "WSL runs seamlessly" guarantee, and doubles as the
23	   DESKTOP_APP_PLAN P0 feasibility spike.** **Do not let the prose oversell this as done until parity is green.**
24	2. **TF + torch in one image segfaults.** *Mitigation:* two single-framework images sharing `/scratch`.
25	3. **The $200 envelope only holds because the Gen-2 VLM is excluded.** What fits in $200 is the
26	   **container/bucket/Gen-0 plumbing + inference**, **not** the owned-VLM retrain the North Star ultimately
27	   wants (6–8-GPU GRPO reference, *"largest open risk, currently unquantified"*). State this plainly to
28	   anyone reading the burn-down; don't conflate "decoupled train+infer on $200" (true for the cheap
29	   classifier) with "train the VLM on $200" (likely false).
30	4. **Free-credit reality.** Cost figures are **approximate — verify before committing.** RunPod's "$200
31	   free" is a myth (~$5). Every "$200 tier" has a catch: **GCP $300/90-day is the only clean win**; AWS $200
32	   is split/earned; Azure $200 is 30-day. **You have real DO $200** — but DO lacks serverless-GPU metering
33	   (see the budget-vs-billing tension, [03](03-PLATFORM-OPTIONS.md) §4).
34	5. **Data-contract break via re-encode.** MD5 keys the whole 3-repo join; the collector's `nvenc`/`libx264`
35	   proxy transcode changes it, and the export `manifest.json` **omits `md5`.** *Mitigation:* **add `md5` to
36	   the collector export + decide original-vs-proxy keying — an M2 prerequisite**, not a footnote.
37	6. **Spot preemption loses local disk.** *Mitigation:* checkpoint train state to the bucket every ≤30 min;
38	   inference jobs are retry-safe.
39	7. **Gemini key custody / cost.** Default segmenter silent-fails without the key (~$0.05/run). *Mitigation:*
40	   inject as a secret; rate-limit per API key so a leak can't drain the budget or someone else's bill.
41	
42	## 2. Legal / scope gates (these can *block*, not just delay)
43	
44	- **Scope re-ratification.** The ratified posture is *"rent nothing until the owned model has a
45	  ship-gate-passing reason"* (`VIDEO_LOCALITY_MODEL.md`), and the harness is *"single-tenant / local-first
46	  … explicitly NOT a service"* (`EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md`). **The North Star requires explicitly overriding
47	  both** — an owner decision, ideally recorded in `docs/archives/decisions/DECISIONS.md`.
48	- **DPA / no-train gate on the *inference-on-uploads* half specifically.** Plans forbid sending non-consented
49	  video to cloud for training (*"only owned/consented footage, never user uploads"*). *Inference* on a
50	  user's upload over a no-train DPA (Gemini via Vertex, zero-retention) is generally fine, **but a
51	  non-owner user uploading footage to your cloud raises consent/processing-DPA questions that must be
52	  answered before M3.** Treat this as a **gating precondition on the upload-inference half**, not a footnote.
53	- **Weight/dataset licensing.** WASB-C3 checkpoint provenance + per-checkpoint weight terms gate shipping any
54	  cloud-trained model commercially (orthogonal to compute portability, but real for the TF-image/M4 epic).
55	
56	## 3. Staleness checks before building (verified where noted)
57	
58	- ✅ **Async control plane exists** (`jobs` table, `job_worker.py`) — confirmed 2026-06-20.
59	- ✅ **Committed synthetic video-free regression fixtures exist** (`golden_fixtures.py`) — M0 is a real $0 gate.
60	- ✅ **`"output"` still hardcoded** (`trajectory_hybrid.py:196,272`) — still a live blocker.
61	- ✅ **Gen-0 harness exists** (`training/gen0/harness.py`).
62	- ⬜ **Confirm `DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md` #16** specifies exactly what the control plane shipped vs deferred
63	  (queue tech is still undecided: Arq vs Postgres-queue vs Hatchet vs Temporal) — not harvested in this pass.
64	- ⬜ **Confirm the collector export manifest `md5` gap** at build time and pick the keying rule (§5 risk).
65	
66	## 4. Decisions for the owner
67	
68	| # | Decision | Recommendation |
69	|---|---|---|
70	| 1 | **Greenlight this as an active project** (override the "rent nothing / not a service" posture for a scoped build)? | Yes, scoped to **inference + Gen-0 train only**; record in DECISIONS.md. |
71	| 2 | **Where does the inference endpoint run** (per-video billing vs DO credits)? | DO for M1–M2 dev/train; **GCP Cloud Run GPU or RunPod Serverless for M3** (native per-invocation metering = the per-video bill). Or single-vendor GCP ($300 trial) for all. |
72	| 3 | **Bucket:** DO Spaces (1 TiB egress incl., uses credits) vs R2 (zero egress) vs GCS (native if GCP)? | DO Spaces for the dev loop; revisit R2 if reel egress grows. |
73	| 4 | **Docker now or VM/venv first?** | venv/SkyPilot for M0–M2; container at M3 (serverless needs it). |
74	| 5 | **DPA/consent posture for non-owner uploads** (gates M3)? | Resolve before M3; inference-only no-train DPA + consent capture. |
75	| 6 | **`md5` in collector export + original-vs-proxy keying** (gates M2)? | Add `md5`; key on the byte-identical file labels were made on. |
76	| 7 | **Confirm the VLM is out of scope for this $200 build?** | Yes — Gen-2 is a separate, separately-budgeted effort. |
77	| 8 | **Ratify the M3.5 WSL→native parity-on-DigitalOcean approach** (drop WSL on Linux, keep it for Windows dev, gate on a CSV parity test)? | Yes — it's the "WSL runs seamlessly" guarantee and doubles as the DESKTOP_APP_PLAN P0 spike. ✅ **Resolved 2026-06-20: $200 credit covers Paperspace GPUs + a free unbilled M4000** (so the parity run is $0; verify the M4000's `sm_52`/Maxwell arch is in your pinned torch build). |
78	
79	## 5. What success looks like
80	
81	A non-owner user `POST`s a video (with an API key), gets back a highlight reel + rally CSV **and a
82	line-item cost for that video**; the owner runs `train` over a bucket and gets a versioned, ship-gated model
83	plus the run's cost segmented per contributing video — **all on rented, scale-to-zero GPU, for well under
84	$200, with zero idle-GPU billing**, and with the WSL bridge either gone (M4) or cleanly deferred behind the
85	container-clean path.
86

2

7	> **What this directory is.** A single, unified place that answers one question: *can we lift the
8	> rally-segmentation pipeline off the owner's local Windows/WSL box and run it on a **rented,
9	> credit-budgeted, cloud-native GPU** — driven by an API key — that does **both** (a) **retrain** the
10	> rally model from a **bucket of videos** and (b) run **inference on new uploads** — and **bill each
11	> video / each training run exactly**, including for a non-owner user?* Short answer: the *design*

13	> provider, a cost ledger) does not. This directory inventories what's planned, maps the real compute,

2	
3	**Status:** **IMPLEMENTED (augmenting), 2026-06-17** — owner-reviewed + decisions locked 2026-06-17;
4	`backend/eval/tolerance_metrics.py` + `rally_seg_eval` reporting landed (default-additive; tIoU stays the

6	A focused increment of the rally-quality project ([RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md](RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md) §7;
7	the day-1 validation that motivates it: [REAL_FOOTAGE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md](REAL_FOOTAGE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md);
8	the directive: `eval-boundary-tolerance` + `eval-dual-set-regression`).
9	
10	> **Implementation refinement (2026-06-17):** §2.3.2's boundary-error distribution is computed over
11	> **unconditional best-overlap** matches, NOT the within-τ 1:1 matches — a τ-gated set is survivorship-biased
12	> (loose ends fail the gate and vanish, hiding the very end-deficit the diagnostic exists to surface; that
13	> within-τ deficit instead shows up as low `tol_recall`). Matches the day-1 validation method. Measured on the
14	> n=9 corpus: **|Δstart| 1.25 s vs |Δend| 2.19 s** — "start≈solved, end=the gap", confirmed.
15	
16	---
17	
18	## 0. TL;DR
19	`tIoU-F1@0.5` is the wrong ruler for rally detection, in **both** directions: it over-penalizes the
20	**intrinsically fuzzy rally START** (the ~1–1.5 s service window), and its overlap matching is murky about
21	**over-production**. R2 replaces the headline with a **tolerance-match metric under strict 1:1 (Hungarian)
22	assignment**, reported as a **decomposition** — `P@τ / R@τ / F1@τ` + **boundary-error distributions split
23	into start/end** + an **over-segmentation guard** + a **τ-sweep** — not a single number.
24	
25	**Honest, slightly uncomfortable consequence (measured, see §3):** under R2 the local detector's headline
26	number *drops* and the gap to Gemini *widens* vs what tIoU showed — because R2 faithfully charges for the
27	1.5× over-production and the loose ends that tIoU's overlap matching partly absorbed. That is the point:
28	R2 splits "behind Gemini" into its two real causes — **(a) over-production [precision]** and **(b) loose
29	rally-ends [the R4 lever]** — and forgives only the inherent start fuzziness. It is a more honest ruler,
30	not a flattering one.
31	
32	---
33	
34	## 1. Why `tIoU@0.5` is the wrong ruler (grounded in the n=2 ground-truth clips)
35	1. **Over-penalizes the START.** For a 5.7 s rally, `tIoU ≥ 0.5` tolerates only ~±1.9 s of boundary shift
36	   (`Δ = D·(1−τ)/(1+τ)`). The service window (stance → racquet contact) alone is ~1–1.5 s — even **Gemini's**
37	   median |Δstart| is 0.98–1.46 s. So tIoU spends most of its budget on a boundary that is *intrinsically*
38	   undefined to ~1 s. (Owner directive: don't strictly align starts; tolerate ~±2 s.)
39	2. **Count parity is a deceptive proxy.** mahadevpura-2 hit 1.03× of Gemini's count yet F1@0.5 = 0.150 —
40	   right count, wrong boundaries. A count- or overlap-based view hides this.
41	3. **It flips config rankings on noise.** The hard-cap was golden-neutral at n=6 (+0.006 n.s.) but "best" at
42	   n=7 once a continuously-active clip entered — a metric artifact at the 0.5 cliff, not a quality change.
43	4. **The deficits we must see are start≠end-symmetric.** Local's |Δstart| ≈ Gemini's; the **entire** gap is
44	   the rally-**END** (|Δend| 2.1–3.1 s vs Gemini ~0.8–1.2 s). A single blended IoU cannot show that — and
45	   directing R4 needs exactly that decomposition.

### 307. assistant (2026-06-21T01:01:42.159Z)

Here's the summary — grounded against the current code (confirmed: **no Dockerfile** exists, the **`jobs` table has tenant `user_id` scaffolding but no cost/billing columns**, and the API is **CORS `*` with no auth**).

# Cloud Run + GPU serving — project summary

**One-liner:** Turn the decoupled `worker` (which already runs train/infer off a GCS bucket on a rented GPU) into a **paid, multi-tenant serving service on Cloud Run + GPU (scale-to-zero)** that bills **per video**.

**Why now / provenance:** It's the productionization successor to DECOUPLED_COMPUTE. DECOUPLED proved *compute portability* (M0–M2 + M3.5 on a GCP L4) and was archived; its **deferred M3 (serving endpoint + per-video billing) + M4 (scale-to-zero + cost guard)** were routed here. Seeded in **ADR-012**, made GCP-only by **ADR-013**, and **unblocked** now that DECOUPLED is archived.

**The shape (architecture):** a thin **control plane** (the existing FastAPI `jobs` API: accept job → report status) in front of a **Cloud Run + GPU data plane** that pulls the video from a `gcs://` bucket, runs `backend.worker infer` on an L4, and writes rallies + reel + report back to the bucket. **Scale-to-zero** means you pay per-invocation GPU-seconds — which *is* the per-video bill — with zero idle-GPU cost.

**What already exists (reuse — the heavy lifting is done):**
- `python -m backend.worker {infer|train}` over `gcs://` (bucket-driven, validated this session).
- Storage backends (`gcs://`/`s3://`/local) + the **M3.5 native WASB runner** (`detector_impl="native"`).
- Async **`jobs` control plane** + **multi-tenant scaffolding** (`user_id` on videos/candidate_segments, a per-user model store).
- GCS bucket + `rally-worker` SA + `$100` budget; the `weights/0.1.0-l4/` bundle; `GET /api/health` + `/api/compile` download_url (just merged, #268).

**What's net-new (the actual build — ADR-012 seed scope):**
1. **Containerize `backend.worker`** — *the headline gap*: no Dockerfile exists, and the WASB **torch-1.11 conda env** (separate from the indexer venv) makes the image non-trivial. Needs an **NVDEC-capable** base.
2. **Cloud Run + GPU (L4) service** running `infer` over a `gcs://` workspace, scale-to-zero.
3. **Per-video cost ledger** on the `jobs` table (today it has tenant columns but **no cost/price columns**) = the per-customer bill (M3).
4. **Cost guard + concurrency caps** (M4).
5. **Edge auth** — the engine is **CORS `*`, no auth**; a tenancy boundary is a hard prerequisite before any public exposure.
6. **NVDEC GPU-decode** — kills the measured ~12 fps cv2 decode bottleneck that starved the L4 during M2.
7. **DPA/consent gate** for non-owner uploads (legal, gates public serving).

**Owner decisions / gates still open:** edge-auth mechanism (Caddy basic-auth vs Cloudflare Access), retention defaults, per-video cost ceiling, **WASB-C3 weight provenance** (gates *sharing* a trained model), DPA/consent posture. **Gen-2 VLM is explicitly out of scope/budget.**

**Economics:** L4 ≈ $0.70–0.85/hr *running*, but scale-to-zero + per-invocation metering = pay only for the GPU-seconds a video actually consumes → exact per-video attribution, no idle burn. This is precisely why Cloud Run+GPU beat DO/Paperspace for the billing model.

**Relationship to the other track:** this is the **compute/billing backend**; the **per-rally selection UI** (khelsutra `web/`) + the appliance design (`LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md`) are the **operator console / front end**. Cloud Run+GPU is the "remote GPU / compute-as-a-setting" scale path of that appliance.

**Status:** **SEEDED, not started.** No tracked project doc yet — it lives only as the ADR-012 seed scope + the carried-forward items in `current-state`.

Want me to **draft the tracked project doc** (`docs/CLOUD_RUN_GPU_SERVING.md` off `PROJECT_DOC_TEMPLATE`, with the §7 P0→P6 progress tracker), and/or render a quick **architecture diagram**? Both are easy next steps whenever you greenlight it.

2	  "core_unaffected_principle": "THE CONTRACT (verbatim, code-grounded). The rally engine is a PURE function of (input bytes + config) \u2192 outputs, split into three deterministic stages that NO deployment profile may alter:\n\n1. DETECT (GPU): DetectorRunner.run_predict(video_path, output_dir, video_id) -> CSV[Frame,Visibility,X,Y] (backend/pipeline/detectors/base.py:164-192). Three interchangeable concrete impls \u2014 WasbRunner (WSL), NativeWasbRunner (Linux GPU), StubDetectorRunner (CPU mock) \u2014 emit the IDENTICAL CSV schema. The trajectory CSV is the stable artifact boundary.\n2. WINDOW (CPU pure fn): TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows(points, fps, frame_width, velocity_thresh, merge_gap, \u2026) -> List[window] (tracknet_runner.py:281-298). A shared @staticmethod with zero GPU/IO/randomness: same trajectory + same config = byte-identical candidate windows. Used by ALL runners and by the eval/regression stack verbatim.\n3. STITCH (CPU/ffmpeg): VideoCompiler.compile_highlights(raw_video_path, segments, output_filename) -> reel path (stitcher/compiler.py:303-414). Pure filesystem+subprocess: _merge_overlapping \u2192 ffprobe duration \u2192 _watermark_filter (built once) \u2192 per-clip libx264 trim+watermark \u2192 concat. Deterministic given the same input video + segment ranges + watermark config; no hardware assumptions.\n\nWHY IT STAYS BYTE/BEHAVIOUR-IDENTICAL ACROSS MODES. The deployment profile NEVER edits these three functions. It only chooses (a) WHICH DetectorRunner subclass is instantiated (the single seam _resolve_runner reading RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL/indexing.detector_impl, trajectory_hybrid.py:65-86) and (b) WHERE input bytes come from / outputs go (StorageBackend ABC, backend/storage/base.py \u2014 fetch_to_local/put). The math (windowing) is shared and seam-free; the detector contract guarantees CSV-schema uniformity so windowing is model- AND location-agnostic; stitching takes a local file + ranges and is identical on any box with ffmpeg. CAVEAT (honest, from the GCP deploy report 2026-06-20): DETECT is byte-identical only on the SAME GPU; cross-GPU (L4 vs RTX-2070) trajectories differ by sub-pixel cuDNN amounts (684/1195 frames). Therefore the parity contract is enforced at the WINDOW + STITCH layer (rally-window equivalence, exact for a fixed trajectory), NOT at detector-CSV bytes across heterogeneous GPUs. The $0 stub path (replay_csv) gives a byte-exact end-to-end anchor on CPU that is identical local-vs-cloud because no real GPU is involved.\n\nNON-GOAL: the decoupling effort must NOT introduce a code branch inside detect/window/stitch keyed on profile. Every profile difference lives in config + the two registries (Compute, Storage). This is the same discipline the experiment harness already enforces (disabled-cue byte-identical-to-CONTROL).",

38	  "compute_placement": "DETECT runs on the GPU in every serving profile (NativeWasbRunner device=cuda on Cloud Run's NVIDIA L4; WasbRunner via WSL or NativeWasbRunner locally). WINDOW is CPU-pure everywhere. STITCH (VideoCompiler, ffmpeg libx264 + optional NVENC) is CPU work.\n\nPlacement by profile:\n- truly-local: detect on the local GPU, stitch in the SAME local process. Free to the operator; nothing to meter.\n- cloud-serving: detect on the Cloud Run GPU job AND stitch on Cloud Run too \u2014 co-located in the same billed invocation (or a same-region Cloud Run CPU job). WHY stitch on Cloud Run (the owner's explicit point): stitching is genuinely compute-intensive (per-clip re-encode + concat, ~minutes per reel) and its CPU/wall-time + egress bytes are a REAL cost; running it on Cloud Run means gpu_active_seconds (detect) + wall_seconds (detect+stitch) + bytes_out (reel egress) are all captured on ONE metered unit and aggregated into the per-job cost ledger (cost_usd + cost_breakdown_json {gpu, cpu, egress, api}), versus running stitch on a laptop where its cost would be invisible/unattributed. Stitch can stay SYNCHRONOUS inside the GPU job for the common case (one reel per video, cheap), with a queued-stitch fallback only if a tenant submits many compiles per video (bursty) \u2014 the job table + claim_due_job already supports both.\n- cpu-mock: detect = stub (CPU), stitch on CPU or skipped; no metering.\n\nCost-ledger hooks (NET-NEW, designed in COST-ATTRIBUTION.md): timestamp probes around the GPU detect stage record gpu_active_seconds; the Cloud Run/serverless platform per-invocation report gives exact billed seconds; wall_seconds wraps detect+stitch; bytes_out from the reel push; gemini metrics already returned by analyze_video(). Sum via a versioned pricebook into the jobs-row cost columns.",
